\displaystyle\lesssim k^{61/32}r^{1/8}\kappa^{-3/16}(\min\{n,R\}-r)^{3/8}\left(\dfrac{C_{2}\kappa^{2}\sqrt{n}}{\sqrt{\min\{n,R\}}}\right)^{21\kappa^{2}}\left(\dfrac{\alpha}{\|\bm{\mathcal{X}}\|}\right)^{21/16},

as desired.

Remark: One could obtain similar results for the cases where
r\leq R<2r
and
2r\leq R<3r
by choosing the parameter
\epsilon\in(0,1)
appropriately.

Appendix G
Restricted Isometry Property

In this section, we show that a measurement operator which satisfies the standard restricted isometry property also satisfies two other variants of the restricted isometry property - a fact which we used in our analysis of the convergence stage.

We say that a measurement operator
\mathcal{A}:S^{n\times n\times k}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
satisfies the spectral-to-spectral Restricted Isometry Property of rank-
r
with constant
\delta>0
(abbreviated
\text{S2SRIP}(r,\delta)
) if for all tensors
\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\in S^{n\times n\times k}
with tubal-rank
\leq r
,

\|(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})\|\leq\delta\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|.

We say that a measurement operator
\mathcal{A}:S^{n\times n\times k}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
satisfies the spectral-to-nuclear Restricted Isometry Property with constant
\delta>0
(abbreviated
\text{S2NRIP}(\delta)
) if for all tensors
\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\in S^{n\times n\times k}
with tubal-rank
\leq r
,

\|(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})\|\leq\delta\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|_{*}.

Lemma G.1
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{A}:S^{n\times n\times k}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
satisfies
\text{RIP}(r+r^{\prime},\delta)
with
0<\delta<1
. Then, for any
\bm{\mathcal{Z}},\bm{\mathcal{Y}}\in S^{n\times n\times k}
with
\operatorname{rank}(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})\leq r
and
\operatorname{rank}(\bm{\mathcal{Y}})\leq r^{\prime}
, we have

\left|\left<(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}}),\bm{\mathcal{Y}}\right>\right|\leq\delta\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|_{F}\|\bm{\mathcal{Y}}\|_{F}.

Proof.

Let
\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}=\tfrac{\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|_{F}}{\|\bm{\mathcal{Y}}\|_{F}}\bm{\mathcal{Y}}
so that
\|\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\|_{F}=\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|_{F}
. Note that
\bm{\mathcal{Z}}+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\in S^{n\times n\times k}
and
\bm{\mathcal{Z}}-\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\in S^{n\times n\times k}
both have tubal rank
\leq r+r^{\prime}
. Then, by using the identities
\|\bm{a}+\bm{b}\|^{2}-\|\bm{a}-\bm{b}\|^{2}=4\left<\bm{a},\bm{b}\right>
and
\|\bm{a}+\bm{b}\|^{2}+\|\bm{a}-\bm{b}\|^{2}=2\|\bm{a}\|^{2}+2\|\bm{b}\|^{2}
(which both hold over any inner product space) along with the fact that
\mathcal{A}
satisfies
\text{RIP}(r+r^{\prime},\delta)
, we have:

\displaystyle\left<(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}}),\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\right>

\displaystyle=\left<\bm{\mathcal{Z}},\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\right>-\left<\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A}(\bm{\mathcal{Z}}),\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\right>

\displaystyle=\left<\bm{\mathcal{Z}},\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\right>-\left<\mathcal{A}(\bm{\mathcal{Z}}),\mathcal{A}(\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})\right>

\displaystyle=\left<\bm{\mathcal{Z}},\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\right>-\dfrac{1}{4}\|\mathcal{A}(\bm{\mathcal{Z}}+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})\|_{2}^{2}+\dfrac{1}{4}\|\mathcal{A}(\bm{\mathcal{Z}}-\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left<\bm{\mathcal{Z}},\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\right>-\dfrac{1}{4}(1-\delta)\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\|_{F}^{2}+\dfrac{1}{4}(1+\delta)\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}-\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left<\bm{\mathcal{Z}},\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\right>-\dfrac{1}{4}\left(\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\|_{F}^{2}-\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}-\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\|_{F}^{2}\right)+\dfrac{1}{4}\delta\left(\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}+\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\|_{F}^{2}+\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}-\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\|_{F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\dfrac{1}{2}\delta\left(\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|_{F}^{2}+\|\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\|_{F}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\delta\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|_{F}\|\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\|_{F}

In a similar manner,
\left<(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}}),\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\right>\geq-\delta\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|_{F}\|\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\|_{F}
. Hence,
\left|\left<(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}}),\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\right>\right|\leq\delta\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|_{F}\|\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\|_{F}
. Then, since
\mathcal{Y}
is a scalar multiple of
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
, we have

\left|\left<(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}}),\bm{\mathcal{Y}}\right>\right|=\tfrac{\|\bm{\mathcal{Y}}\|_{F}}{\|\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\|_{F}}\left|\left<(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}}),\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\right>\right|\leq\tfrac{\|\bm{\mathcal{Y}}\|_{F}}{\|\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\|_{F}}\delta\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|_{F}\|\bm{\mathcal{Y}}^{\prime}\|_{F}=\delta\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|_{F}\|\bm{\mathcal{Y}}\|_{F}.

∎

Lemma G.2
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{A}:S^{n\times n\times k}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
satisfies
\text{RIP}(r+1,\delta_{1})
, where
0<\delta_{1}<1
. Then,
\mathcal{A}
also satisfies
\text{S2SRIP}(r,\sqrt{kr}\delta_{1})
.

Proof.

Suppose
\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\in S^{n\times n\times k}
has tubal-rank
r
. Since
(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})
is symmetric, its t-SVD is of the form

(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})=\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})}*\bm{\Sigma}_{(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})}*\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})}^{\top}.

Now, define
\bm{\mathcal{V}}=\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})}(:,1,:)\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times 1\times k}
and let
\bm{s}\in\mathbb{R}^{1\times 1\times k}
be defined by
\bm{s}(1,1,\ell)=\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{k}}e^{\sqrt{-1}2\pi j\ell}
where
j=\argmax_{j^{\prime}}|\widehat{\bm{\Sigma}}(1,1,j^{\prime})|
. With this definition, one can check that
\left|\left<(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}}),\bm{\mathcal{V}}*\bm{s}*\bm{\mathcal{V}}^{\top}\right>\right|=\|(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})\|
. Then, since
\mathcal{A}
satisfies
\text{RIP}(r+1,\delta_{1})
and
\operatorname{rank}(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})\leq r
and
\operatorname{rank}(\bm{\mathcal{V}}*\bm{s}*\bm{\mathcal{V}}^{\top})=1
, by Lemma
G.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})\|

\displaystyle=\left|\left<(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}}),\bm{\mathcal{V}}*\bm{s}*\bm{\mathcal{V}}^{\top}\right>\right|

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{1}\|\bm{\mathcal{V}}*\bm{s}*\bm{\mathcal{V}}^{\top}\|_{F}\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\delta_{1}\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{1}\sqrt{kr}\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|.

Since the bound
\|(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})\|\leq\delta_{1}\sqrt{kr}\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|
holds for any
\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\in S^{n\times n\times k}
with tubal rank
\leq r
, we have that
\mathcal{A}
satisfies
\text{S2SRIP}(r,\sqrt{kr}\delta_{1})
.
∎

Lemma G.3
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{A}:S^{n\times n\times k}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
satisfies
\text{RIP}(2,\delta_{2})
where
0<\delta_{2}<1
. Then,
\mathcal{A}
also satisfies
\text{S2NRIP}(\sqrt{k}\delta_{2})
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{A}
satisfies
\text{RIP}(2,\delta_{2})
, by Lemma
G.2
for
r=1
,
\mathcal{A}
satisfies
\text{S2SRIP}(1,\sqrt{k}\delta_{2})
. Now, suppose that
\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\in S^{n\times n\times k}
. Since
\bm{\mathcal{Z}}
is symmetric, it has a t-SVD in the form

\bm{\mathcal{Z}}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{i}*\bm{s}_{i}*\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{i}^{\top}.

Then, since each term
\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{i}*\bm{s}_{i}*\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{i}^{\top}
is symmetric with tubal rank
1
, we have

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})\|

\displaystyle=\left\|(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{i}*\bm{s}_{i}*\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{i}^{\top}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})\left(\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{i}*\bm{s}_{i}*\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{i}^{\top}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left\|(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})\left(\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{i}*\bm{s}_{i}*\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{i}^{\top}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sqrt{k}\delta_{2}\left\|\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{i}*\bm{s}_{i}*\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{i}^{\top}\right\|

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sqrt{k}\delta_{2}\left\|\bm{s}_{i}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{k}\delta_{2}\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|_{*}

Since the bound
\|(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})\|\leq\sqrt{k}\delta_{2}\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|_{*}
holds for any
\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\in S^{n\times n\times k}
, we have that
\mathcal{A}
satisfies
\text{S2NRIP}(\sqrt{k}\delta_{2})
.
∎

Lemma G.4
.

Suppose
\mathcal{A}:S^{n\times n\times k}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
satisfies
\text{RIP}(2r,\delta_{3})
, where
0<\delta_{3}<1
, and
\bm{\mathcal{V}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times r\times k}
satisfies
\bm{\mathcal{V}}^{\top}*\bm{\mathcal{V}}=\bm{\mathcal{I}}
. Then, for any
\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\in S^{n\times n\times k}
with
\operatorname{rank}(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})\leq r
, we have

\left\|\bm{\mathcal{V}}^{\top}*\left[(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})\right]\right\|_{F}\leq\delta_{3}\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|_{F}.

Proof.

Let
\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\in S^{n\times n\times k}
, and let
\bm{\mathcal{Y}}=\tfrac{\bm{\mathcal{V}}^{\top}*\left[(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})\right]}{\|\bm{\mathcal{V}}^{\top}*\left[(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})\right]\|_{F}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times n\times k}
. Trivially,
\|\bm{\mathcal{Y}}\|_{F}=1
, and so,
\|\bm{\mathcal{V}}*\bm{\mathcal{Y}}\|_{F}^{2}=\left<\bm{\mathcal{V}}*\bm{\mathcal{Y}},\bm{\mathcal{V}}*\bm{\mathcal{Y}}\right>=\left<\bm{\mathcal{Y}},\bm{\mathcal{V}}^{\top}*\bm{\mathcal{V}}*\bm{\mathcal{Y}}\right>=\left<\bm{\mathcal{Y}},\bm{\mathcal{Y}}\right>=\|\bm{\mathcal{Y}}\|_{F}^{2}=1
. Then, by using Lemma
G.1
, we have that

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\mathcal{V}}^{\top}*\left[(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})\right]\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\left<\bm{\mathcal{V}}^{\top}*\left[(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})\right],\bm{\mathcal{Y}}\right>

\displaystyle=\left<\left[(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A})(\bm{\mathcal{Z}})\right],\bm{\mathcal{V}}*\bm{\mathcal{Y}}\right>

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{3}\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|_{F}\|\bm{\mathcal{V}}*\bm{\mathcal{Y}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle=\delta_{3}\|\bm{\mathcal{Z}}\|_{F}

∎

Appendix H
Properties of Aligned Matrix Subspaces

In this section, we collect some properties of matrices and their subspaces, useful for the proof of the results in the tensor Fourier domain.

Lemma H.1
.

(
[
36
]
)
For some orthogonal matrix
X\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times r}
and some full-rank matrix
Y\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times R}

consider
X^{\mathrm{H}}Y=V\Sigma W^{\mathrm{H}}
, and the following decomposition of
Y

Y=YWW^{\mathrm{H}}+YW_{\perp}W_{\perp}^{\mathrm{H}}

(H.1)

with its SVD decomposition

Y=\sum_{i=1}^{R}\sigma_{i}u_{i}v_{i}^{\mathrm{H}}

and the best rank-
r
approximation

Y_{r}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\sigma_{i}u_{i}v_{i}^{\mathrm{H}}

.
Then if the distance between the column subspace of
Y_{r}
and the subspace spanned by the columns of
X
is small enough, that is
\|X_{\perp}^{\mathrm{H}}V_{Y_{r}}\|\leq\frac{1}{8}
, then the decomposition (
H.1
) follows some low-rank approximation properties, namely

\displaystyle\|X_{\perp}^{\mathrm{H}}V_{YW}\|

\displaystyle\leq 7\|X_{\perp}^{\mathrm{H}}V_{Y_{r}}\|

(H.2)

\displaystyle\|YW_{\perp}\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sigma_{r+1}(Y).

(H.3)

Lemma H.2
.

For a matrix
X\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times r}
,
r\leq n
, with its SVD-decomposition
X=V_{X}\Sigma_{X}W_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}
and some a full-rank matrix
Y\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times R}
,
consider
V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}Y=V\Sigma W^{\mathrm{H}}
, and the following decomposition of
Y

Y=YWW^{\mathrm{H}}+YW_{\perp}W_{\perp}^{\mathrm{H}}.

(H.4)

Let matrix
H\in\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}
be defined as

H=V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}(\mathrm{Id}+\mu Z)YW

with some
Z\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
,
parameter
\mu\leq\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\|V^{\mathrm{H}}Y\|^{-2}
and
\|V^{\mathrm{H}}_{\perp}V_{YW}\|\leq c_{2}
with sufficiently small constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
. Then
H
can be represented as follows

H=(\mathrm{Id}+\mu\Sigma_{X}^{2}-\mu P_{1}+\mu P_{2}+\mu^{2}P_{3})V_{X}YW(\mathrm{Id}-\mu W^{\mathrm{H}}Y^{\mathrm{H}}V_{X}V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}YW)

with matrices
P_{1},P_{2},P_{3}\in\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}
such that

\displaystyle P_{1}:

\displaystyle=V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}YY^{\mathrm{H}}V_{X^{\perp}}V_{X^{\perp}}^{\mathrm{H}}V_{YW}(VV_{YW})^{-1}(\mathrm{Id}-\mu V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}YY^{\mathrm{H}}V_{X})^{-1}

\displaystyle P_{2}:

\displaystyle=V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}(Z-XX^{\mathrm{H}}+YY^{\mathrm{H}})V_{YW}(V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}V_{YW})^{-1}(\mathrm{Id}-\mu V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}YWW^{\mathrm{H}}Y^{\mathrm{H}}V_{X})^{-1}

\displaystyle P_{3}:

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{X}^{2}V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}YW(\mathrm{Id}-\mu W^{\mathrm{H}}Y^{\mathrm{H}}V_{X}V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}YW)^{-1}W^{\mathrm{H}}Y^{\mathrm{H}}V_{X}

with

\displaystyle\|P_{1}\|

\displaystyle\leq 4\|YW\|^{2}\|V_{X^{\perp}}V_{YW}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\|P_{2}\|

\displaystyle\leq 4\|Z-XX^{\mathrm{H}}+YY^{\mathrm{H}}\|

\displaystyle\|P_{3}\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\|X\|^{2}\|YW\|^{2}.

Moreover, it holds that

\sigma_{min}(H)\geq\big(1+\mu\sigma_{min}^{2}(X)-\mu\|P_{1}\|-\mu\|P_{2}\|-\mu^{2}\|P_{3}\|\big)\sigma_{min}(V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}Y)\big(1-\mu\sigma_{min}^{2}(V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}Y)\big).

Proof.

The proof of this Lemma follows from Lemma 9.1 in
[
36
]
by using an independent matrix
Z\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
instead of the matrix
\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A}(XX^{\mathrm{H}}-YY^{\mathrm{H}})
, omitting the assumption
\|Y\|\leq 3\|X\|
and updating respectively the transformation steps.
∎

Lemma H.3
.

For a matrix
X\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times r}
,
r\leq n
with its SVD-decomposition
X=V_{X}\Sigma_{X}W_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}
and some full-rank matrix
Y\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times R}
and
Y_{1}=(\mathrm{Id}+\mu Z)Y

consider
V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}Y=V\Sigma W^{\mathrm{H}}
,
V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}Y_{1}=V_{1}\Sigma_{1}W_{1}^{\mathrm{H}}
, and the following decomposition of
Y
and
Y_{1}

\displaystyle Y

\displaystyle=YWW^{\mathrm{H}}+YW_{\perp}W_{\perp}^{\mathrm{H}}.

\displaystyle Y_{1}

\displaystyle=Y_{1}W_{1}W_{1}^{\mathrm{H}}+Y_{1}W_{1,\perp}W_{1,\perp}^{\mathrm{H}}.

Assume that
V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}Y_{1}W
is invertible, which also implies that
Y_{1}W
is has full-rank, and that
{\|V_{X^{\perp}}^{\mathrm{H}}V_{Y_{1}W}\|\leq\frac{1}{50}}
and
\mu\leq\min{\left\{\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{3}}\|V_{X^{\perp}}^{\mathrm{H}}YW_{\perp}\|^{-2},\tfrac{1}{9}\|X\|^{-2}\right\}}
and moreover,
\mu
is small enough so that
{0\preceq\mathrm{Id}-\mu V_{X^{\perp}}^{\mathrm{H}}YWW^{\mathrm{H}}Y^{\mathrm{H}}V_{X^{\perp}}\preceq\mathrm{Id}}
. Consider two matrices

\displaystyle G_{1}:

\displaystyle=-V_{X^{\perp}}^{\mathrm{H}}Y_{1}W(V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}Y_{1}W)^{-1}V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}Y_{1}W_{\perp}W_{\perp}^{\mathrm{H}}W_{1,\perp}

\displaystyle G_{2}:

\displaystyle=V_{X^{\perp}}^{\mathrm{H}}Y_{1}W_{\perp}W_{\perp}^{\mathrm{H}}W_{1,\perp}.

Then these matrices
can be represented as

G_{1}=\mu V_{X^{\perp}}^{\mathrm{H}}V_{Y_{1}W}(V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}V_{Y_{1}W})^{-1}M_{1}V_{X^{\perp}}^{\mathrm{H}}YW_{\perp}W_{\perp}^{\mathrm{H}}W_{1,\perp}

with
M_{1}:=V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}(ZV_{X^{\perp}}-XX^{\mathrm{H}}V_{X^{\perp}})

and

\displaystyle G_{2}

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathrm{Id}-\mu M_{2}+\mu M_{3})V_{X^{\perp}}^{\mathrm{H}}YW_{\perp}(\mathrm{Id}-\mu W_{\perp}^{\mathrm{H}}Y^{\mathrm{H}}YW_{\perp})-\mu^{2}(M_{2}-M_{3})V_{X^{\perp}}^{\mathrm{H}}YW_{\perp}W_{\perp}^{\mathrm{H}}Y^{\mathrm{H}}YW_{\perp}\Big)\cdot

\displaystyle\quad\quad\cdot W_{\perp}^{\mathrm{H}}W_{1,\perp}

with
M_{2}=V_{X^{\perp}}^{\mathrm{H}}YWW^{\mathrm{H}}Y^{\mathrm{H}}V_{X^{\perp}}
and
M_{3}:=V_{X^{\perp}}^{\mathrm{H}}(Z-(XX^{\mathrm{H}}-YY^{\mathrm{H}}))V_{X^{\perp}}
. Moreover, the norm of
G_{1}
and
G_{2}
can be bounded respectively as

\displaystyle\|G_{1}\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\mu(\|V_{X^{\perp}}^{\mathrm{H}}V_{YW}\|\|YW\|^{2}+\|Z-(XX^{\mathrm{H}}-YY^{\mathrm{H}})\|)\|V_{X^{\perp}}^{\mathrm{H}}V_{Y_{1}W}\|\|YW_{\perp}\|,

\displaystyle\|G_{2}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|YW_{\perp}\|\Big(1-\mu\|YW_{\perp}\|^{2}+\mu\|Z-(XX^{\mathrm{H}}-YY^{\mathrm{H}})\|\Big)

\displaystyle+\mu^{2}\Big(\|YW\|^{2}+\|Z-(XX^{\mathrm{H}}-YY^{\mathrm{H}})\|\Big)\|YW_{\perp}\|^{3}.

Proof.

The proof of this Lemma follows from Lemma 9.2 in
[
36
]
by changing the matrix
\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A}(XX^{\mathrm{H}}-YY^{\mathrm{H}})
to the independent matrix
Z\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
and taking into account the respective changes without having the condition
\|Y\|\leq 3\|X\|
.
∎

Lemma H.4
.

For a matrix
X\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times r}
,
r\leq n
with its SVD-decomposition
X=V_{X}\Sigma_{X}W_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}
and some full-rank matrix
Y\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times R}
and
Y_{1}:=(\mathrm{Id}+\mu Z)Y

consider
V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}Y=V\Sigma W^{\mathrm{H}}
,
V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}Y_{1}=V_{1}\Sigma_{1}W_{1}^{\mathrm{H}}
, and the following decomposition of
Y
and
Y_{1}

\displaystyle Y

\displaystyle=YWW^{\mathrm{H}}+YW_{\perp}W_{\perp}^{\mathrm{H}},

\displaystyle Y_{1}

\displaystyle=Y_{1}W_{1}W_{1}^{\mathrm{H}}+Y_{1}W_{1,\perp}W_{1,\perp}^{\mathrm{H}}.

Then it holds that

\displaystyle\|W_{\perp}^{\mathrm{H}}W_{1}\|

\displaystyle\leq\mu\left(1+\mu\frac{\|Z\|\|YW\|}{\sigma_{\text{min}}(V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}Y)}\right)\|YW\|\|YW_{\perp}\|\|V_{X^{\perp}}^{\mathrm{H}}V_{YW}\|+\mu\frac{\|Z-(XX^{\mathrm{H}}-YY^{\mathrm{H}})\|}{\sigma_{\text{min}}(V_{X}^{\mathrm{H}}Y)}\|YW_{\perp}\|

(H.5)

Moreover, if for
P:=YW_{\perp}W_{\perp}^{\mathrm{H}}W_{1}(V_{YW}^{\mathrm{H}}YWW^{\mathrm{H}}W_{1})^{-1}V_{YW}^{\mathrm{H}}
the following applies

\|\mu Z+P+\mu ZP\|\leq 1,

then it holds that

\displaystyle\|V_{X^{\perp}}^{\mathrm{H}}V_{Y_{1}W_{1}}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|V_{X^{\perp}}^{\mathrm{H}}V_{YW}\|\left(1-\frac{\mu}{2}\sigma_{\text{min}}^{2}(X)+\mu\|YW_{\perp}\|\right)+\mu\|Z-(XX^{\mathrm{H}}-YY^{\mathrm{H}})\|

\displaystyle+\left(1+\mu\|Z\|\right)\frac{2\|W^{\mathrm{H}}_{\perp}W_{1}\|\|YW_{\perp}\|}{\sigma_{\text{min}}(W^{\mathrm{H}}W_{1})\sigma_{\text{min}}(YW)}

(H.6)

\displaystyle+57\left(\mu\|Z\|+(1+\mu\|Z\|)\frac{\|W^{\mathrm{H}}_{\perp}W_{1}\|\|YW_{\perp}\|}{\sigma_{\text{min}}(W^{\mathrm{H}}W_{1})\sigma_{\text{min}}(YW)}\right)^{2}

Proof.

The proof of inequality (
H.5
) follows from the first part of the proof of Lemma B.3 in
[
36
]
. For this one needs to change the matrix
\mathcal{A}^{*}\mathcal{A}(XX^{\mathrm{H}}-YY^{\mathrm{H}})
in
[
36
]
to an independent matrix
Z\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times n}
and take into account the above-given decomposition of matrices
Y
and
Y_{1}
and lack of assumptions on
\mu
and the norm of matrix
Z
. Inequality (
H.6
) follows from the proof of Lemma 9.3 in
[
36
]
. ∎

Appendix I
Random Tubal Tensors

In this section, we derive bounds on the minimum and maximum singular values as well as the Frobenius norm of a random tubal tensor with i.i.d. Gaussian random entries. In our analysis of the spectral stage, we applied these lemmas to the small random initialization.

We start with the following proposition from Rudelson and Vershynin (2009), which bounds the smallest singular value of an
r\times R
random real Gaussian matrix.

Proposition I.1

(
[
33
]
)
.

Let
\bm{G}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times R}
with
r\leq R
have i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
entries. Then, for every
\epsilon>0
, we have

\sigma_{\text{min}}(\bm{G})\geq\epsilon(\sqrt{R}-\sqrt{r-1})

with probability at least
1-(C\epsilon)^{R-r+1}-e^{-cR}
. The constants
C,c>0
are universal.

Also, the following proposition from Tao and Vu (2010) bounds the smallest singular value of an
r\times r
random complex Gaussian matrix.

Proposition I.2

(
[
37
]
)
.

Let
\bm{G}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
have i.i.d.
\mathcal{CN}(0,1)
entries. Then, for every
\epsilon>0
, we have

\sigma_{\text{min}}(\bm{G})\geq\dfrac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{r}}

with probability at least
1-\epsilon^{2}
.

Using these propositions, we can obtain a bound on the smallest singular value of an
r\times R
random complex Gaussian matrix, provided that
r\leq R
.

Lemma I.1
.

Let
\bm{G}\in\mathbb{C}^{r\times R}
with
r\leq R
have i.i.d.
\mathcal{CN}(0,1)
entries. Then, for every
\epsilon>0
, we have

\sigma_{\text{min}}(\bm{G})\geq\begin{cases}\epsilon(\sqrt{R}-\sqrt{2r-1})&\text{if }R>2r\\
\dfrac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{r}}&\text{if }r\leq R\leq 2r\end{cases}

with probability at least

\begin{cases}1-(C\epsilon)^{R-2r+1}-e^{-cR}&\text{if }R>2r\\
1-\epsilon^{2}&\text{if }r\leq R\leq 2r\end{cases}.

The constants
C,c>0
are universal.

Proof.

First, suppose
R>2r
. Let
\bm{G}=\bm{U}\bm{\Sigma}\bm{V}^{H}
be the SVD of
\bm{G}
where
\bm{U}\in\mathbb{C}^{r\times r}
and
\bm{V}\in\mathbb{C}^{R\times R}
are unitary and
\bm{\Sigma}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times R}
. Then, the following real
2r\times 2R
matrix has a real SVD of

\begin{bmatrix}\operatorname{Re}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}&-\operatorname{Im}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}\\
\operatorname{Im}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}&\operatorname{Re}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\operatorname{Re}\left\{\bm{U}\right\}&-\operatorname{Im}\left\{\bm{U}\right\}\\
\operatorname{Im}\left\{\bm{U}\right\}&\operatorname{Re}\left\{\bm{U}\right\}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\bm{\Sigma}&0\\
0&\bm{\Sigma}\end{bmatrix}\begin{bmatrix}\operatorname{Re}\left\{\bm{V}\right\}&-\operatorname{Im}\left\{\bm{V}\right\}\\
\operatorname{Im}\left\{\bm{V}\right\}&\operatorname{Re}\left\{\bm{V}\right\}\end{bmatrix}^{T}.

By using the fact that for any
\bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times q}
with
p\leq q
,
\sigma_{\text{min}}(\bm{A})^{2}=\displaystyle\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}\\
\|\bm{x}\|_{2}=1\end{subarray}}\|\bm{A}^{T}\bm{x}\|_{2}^{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{\text{min}}(\bm{G})^{2}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\text{min}}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\operatorname{Re}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}&-\operatorname{Im}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}\\
\operatorname{Im}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}&\operatorname{Re}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}\end{bmatrix}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{2r}\\
\|\bm{x}\|_{2}=1\end{subarray}}\left\|\begin{bmatrix}\operatorname{Re}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}^{T}&\operatorname{Im}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}^{T}\\
-\operatorname{Im}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}^{T}&\operatorname{Re}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}^{T}\end{bmatrix}\bm{x}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{2r}\\
\|\bm{x}\|_{2}=1\end{subarray}}\left[\left\|\begin{bmatrix}\operatorname{Re}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}^{T}&\operatorname{Im}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}^{T}\end{bmatrix}\bm{x}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|\begin{bmatrix}-\operatorname{Im}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}^{T}&\operatorname{Re}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}^{T}\end{bmatrix}\bm{x}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{2r}\\
\|\bm{x}\|_{2}=1\end{subarray}}\left\|\begin{bmatrix}\operatorname{Re}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}^{T}&\operatorname{Im}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}^{T}\end{bmatrix}\bm{x}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{2r}\\
\|\bm{x}\|_{2}=1\end{subarray}}\left\|\begin{bmatrix}\operatorname{Im}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}^{T}&\operatorname{Re}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}^{T}\end{bmatrix}\bm{x}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sigma_{\text{min}}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\operatorname{Re}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}\\
\operatorname{Im}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}\end{bmatrix}\right)^{2}+\sigma_{\text{min}}\left(\begin{bmatrix}-\operatorname{Im}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}\\
\operatorname{Re}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}\end{bmatrix}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=2\sigma_{\text{min}}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\operatorname{Re}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}\\
\operatorname{Im}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}\end{bmatrix}\right)^{2},

where the last line follows since reordering the rows of a matrix or flipping the sign of some rows doesn’t change the singular values.

Since
\bm{G}\in\mathbb{C}^{r\times R}
has i.i.d.
\mathcal{CN}(0,1)
entries,
\sqrt{2}\begin{bmatrix}\operatorname{Re}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}\\
\operatorname{Im}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{2r\times R}
has i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,1)
entries. Therefore, by Proposition
I.1
, we have that

\sigma_{\text{min}}(\bm{G})\geq\sigma_{\text{min}}\left(\sqrt{2}\begin{bmatrix}\operatorname{Re}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}\\
\operatorname{Im}\left\{\bm{G}\right\}\end{bmatrix}\right)\geq\epsilon(\sqrt{R}-\sqrt{2r-1})

with probability at least
1-(C\epsilon)^{R-2r+1}-e^{-cR}
, as desired.

Next, suppose
r\leq R\leq 2r
. Let
\bm{G}_{r\times r}
be an
r\times r
submatrix of
\bm{G}
. Then,

\sigma_{\text{min}}(\bm{G})^{2}=\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{x}\in\mathbb{C}^{r}\\
\|\bm{x}\|_{2}=1\end{subarray}}\|\bm{G}^{H}\bm{x}\|_{2}^{2}\geq\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{x}\in\mathbb{C}^{r}\\
\|\bm{x}\|_{2}=1\end{subarray}}\|\bm{G}_{r\times r}^{H}\bm{x}\|_{2}^{2}=\sigma_{\text{min}}(\bm{G}_{r\times r})^{2}.

Hence, by Proposition
I.2
, we have

\sigma_{\text{min}}(\bm{G})\geq\sigma_{\text{min}}(\bm{G}_{r\times r})\geq\dfrac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{r}}

with probability at least
1-\epsilon^{2}
.
∎

Using the above lemma, we can bound the smallest singular value of an
r\times R\times k
tubal tensor.

Lemma I.2
.

Let
\bm{\mathcal{G}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times R\times k}
with
r\leq R
have i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,\tfrac{1}{R})
entries. Then, for every
\epsilon>0
, we have

\sigma_{\text{min}}(\bm{G})\geq\begin{cases}\dfrac{\epsilon\sqrt{k}(\sqrt{R}-\sqrt{2r-1})}{\sqrt{R}}&\text{if }R>2r\\
\dfrac{\epsilon\sqrt{k}}{\sqrt{rR}}&\text{if }r\leq R\leq 2r\end{cases}

with probability at least

\begin{cases}1-k(C\epsilon)^{R-2r+1}-ke^{-cR}&\text{if }R>2r\\
1-k\epsilon^{2}&\text{if }r\leq R\leq 2r\end{cases}.

Proof.

Since the entries of
\bm{\mathcal{G}}
are i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,\tfrac{1}{R})
, the entries of
\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{G}}}
are i.i.d.
\mathcal{CN}(0,\tfrac{k}{R})
. Hence, each scaled slice
\sqrt{\tfrac{R}{k}}\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{G}}}^{(j)}\in\mathbb{C}^{r\times R}
for
j=1,\ldots,k
has i.i.d.
\mathcal{CN}(0,1)
entries. By Lemma
I.1
, each scaled slice satisfies

\sigma_{\text{min}}\left(\sqrt{\tfrac{R}{k}}\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{G}}}^{(j)}\right)\geq\begin{cases}\epsilon(\sqrt{R}-\sqrt{2r-1})&\text{if }R>2r\\
\dfrac{\epsilon}{\sqrt{r}}&\text{if }r\leq R\leq 2r\end{cases}

with probability at least

\begin{cases}1-(C\epsilon)^{R-2r+1}-e^{-cR}&\text{if }R>2r\\
1-\epsilon^{2}&\text{if }r\leq R\leq 2r\end{cases}.

Then, by taking a union bound, we have that

\sigma_{\text{min}}(\bm{\mathcal{G}})=\min_{1\leq j\leq k}\sigma_{\text{min}}\left(\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{G}}}^{(j)}\right)\geq\begin{cases}\dfrac{\epsilon\sqrt{k}(\sqrt{R}-\sqrt{2r-1})}{\sqrt{R}}&\text{if }R>2r\\
\dfrac{\epsilon\sqrt{k}}{\sqrt{rR}}&\text{if }r\leq R\leq 2r\end{cases}

with probability at least

\begin{cases}1-k(C\epsilon)^{R-2r+1}-ke^{-cR}&\text{if }R>2r\\
1-k\epsilon^{2}&\text{if }r\leq R\leq 2r\end{cases}.

∎

The following proposition bounds the operator norm of an
r\times R
random Gaussian matrix.

Proposition I.3

(
[
39
]
)
.

Let
\bm{U}\in\mathbb{C}^{n\times R}
have i.i.d.
\mathcal{CN}(0,1)
entries. Then, with probability at least
1-O(e^{-c\max\{n,R\}})
, we have

\|\bm{U}\|\lesssim\sqrt{\max\{n,R\}}.

Using the above proposition, we can bound the norm of an
n\times R\times k
random Gaussian tubal tensor.

Lemma I.3
.

Let
\bm{\mathcal{U}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times R\times k}
have i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,\tfrac{1}{R})
entries. Then, with probability at least
1-O(ke^{-c\max\{n,R\}})
, we have

\|\bm{\mathcal{U}}\|\lesssim\sqrt{\dfrac{k\max\{n,R\}}{R}}.

Proof.

Since the entries of
\bm{\mathcal{U}}
are i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,\tfrac{1}{R})
, the entries of
\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{U}}}
are i.i.d.
\mathcal{CN}(0,\tfrac{k}{R})
. Hence, each scaled slice
\sqrt{\tfrac{R}{k}}\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{U}}}^{(j)}\in\mathbb{C}^{r\times R}
for
j=1,\ldots,k
has i.i.d
\mathcal{CN}(0,1)
entries. By Proposition
I.3
, each scaled slice satisfies

\left\|\sqrt{\tfrac{R}{k}}\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{U}}}^{(j)}\right\|\lesssim\sqrt{\max\{n,R\}}

with probability at least
1-O(e^{-c\max\{n,R\}})
. Then, by taking a union bound, we have that

\|\bm{\mathcal{U}}\|=\max_{1\leq j\leq k}\left\|\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{U}}}^{(j)}\right\|\lesssim\sqrt{\dfrac{k\max\{n,R\}}{R}}

with probability at least
1-O(ke^{-c\max\{n,R\}})
.
∎

Lemma I.4
.

Let
\bm{\mathcal{U}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times R\times k}
have i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,\tfrac{1}{R})
entries. Then, for any fixed
\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times 1\times k}
with
\|\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{1}\|=1
, we have

\|\bm{\mathcal{U}}^{\top}*\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{1}\|_{F}\asymp\sqrt{k}

with probability at least
1-O(ke^{-cR})
.

Proof.

Since the entries of
\bm{\mathcal{U}}
are i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,\tfrac{1}{R})
, the entries of
\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{U}}}
are i.i.d.
\mathcal{CN}(0,\tfrac{k}{R})
, and thus, the entries of
\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{U}}}^{\top}
are also i.i.d.
\mathcal{CN}(0,\tfrac{k}{R})
. Then, for each slice
j=1,\ldots,k
, each entry of the matrix-vector product
\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{U}}^{\top}}^{(j)}\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{V}}}_{1}^{(j)}\in\mathbb{C}^{R}
is i.i.d.
\mathcal{CN}(0,\tfrac{k}{R}\|\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{V}}}_{1}^{(j)}\|_{F}^{2})
. Hence, the quantity

\dfrac{2R}{k}\dfrac{\left\|\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{U}}^{\top}}^{(j)}\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{V}}}_{1}^{(j)}\right\|_{F}^{2}}{\left\|\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{V}}}_{1}^{(j)}\right\|_{F}^{2}}

has a
\chi^{2}(2R)
distribution. It follows that

\left\|\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{U}}^{\top}}^{(j)}\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{V}}}_{1}^{(j)}\right\|_{F}^{2}\asymp k\left\|\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{V}}}_{1}^{(j)}\right\|_{F}^{2}

holds with probability at least
1-O(e^{-cR})
. By taking a union bound over all
j=1,\ldots,k
, we get that

\left\|\bm{\mathcal{U}}^{\top}*\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{1}\right\|_{F}^{2}=\dfrac{1}{k}\left\|\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{U}}^{\top}}\odot\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{V}}}_{1}\right\|_{F}^{2}=\dfrac{1}{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left\|\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{U}}^{\top}}^{(j)}\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{V}}}_{1}^{(j)}\right\|_{F}^{2}\asymp\sum_{j=1}^{k}\left\|\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{V}}}_{1}^{(j)}\right\|_{F}^{2}=\left\|\widetilde{\bm{\mathcal{V}}}_{1}\right\|_{F}^{2}=k\left\|\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{1}\right\|_{F}^{2}=k,

i.e.,
\|\bm{\mathcal{U}}^{\top}*\bm{\mathcal{V}}_{1}\|_{F}\asymp\sqrt{k}
with probability at least
1-O(ke^{-cR})
.
∎
